for large images\n            const retryInterval = 100;\n\n            let containerSized = false;\n\n            const check = () => {\n                try {\n                    if (!containerSized) {\n                        const dimsResult = window.Android.checkAndGetImageDimensions();\n                        const parsed = safeParseJson(dimsResult);\n                        console.error('EmbedSdkLogs: Parsed dimensions:', JSON.stringify(parsed));\n                        if (parsed && parsed.valid && parsed.width && parsed.height) {\n                            sizeImageContainer(parsed.width, parsed.height);\n                            containerSized = true;\n                        }\n                    }\n\n                    if (containerSized && retryInterval);\n                } else {\n                    console.error('Failed to load user image: Rendition not available in time');\n                }\n            };\n\n            check();\n        }\n    } catch (error) {\n        console.error('Error loading user image:', error);\n    }\n}\n\nfunction safeParseJson(str) {\n    try { return JSON.parse(str); } catch { return null; }\n}\n\nfunction sizeImageContainer(originalWidth, originalHeight) {\n    const userImage = document.getElementById(\"user-image\");\n    const container = document.querySelector(\".image-container\");\n    if (!container) return;\n\n    container.style.display = \"flex\";\n    container.classList.add(\"loading\");\n\n    if (userImage) {\n        // Keep the image hidden until ready; container shows shimmer via CSS\n        userImage.style.display = \"none\";\n\n        // Pre-size the image element to match the artboard's \"fit\" dimensions so the\n        // shimmer placeholder visually matches the final image footprint and avoids a\n        // layout jump when the rendition swaps in.\n        if (originalWidth > 0 && originalHeight > 0) {\n            userImage.dataset.originalWidth = String(originalWidth);\n            userImage.dataset.originalHeight = String(originalHeight);\n            applyFittedImageSize(userImage, container, originalWidth, originalHeight);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/**\n * Mirrors Horizon's `ViewportDisplayStrategy.fit` for the loader image: scales the\n * image up or down so it fills the available canvas area while preserving aspect ratio.\n * Sets explicit pixel width/height so the box-shadow follows the visible image bounds.\n */\nfunction applyFittedImageSize(userImage, container, originalWidth, originalHeight) {\n    if (!userImage || !container || !originalWidth || !originalHeight) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const containerStyle = window.getComputedStyle(container);\n    const padLeft = parseFloat(containerStyle.paddingLeft) || 0;\n    const padRight = parseFloat(containerStyle.paddingRight) || 0;\n    const padTop = parseFloat(containerStyle.paddingTop) || 0;\n    const padBottom = parseFloat(containerStyle.paddingBottom) || 0;\n\n    const availableWidth = Math.max(0, container.clientWidth - padLeft - padRight);\n    const availableHeight = Math.max(0, container.clientHeight - padTop - padBottom);\n\n    if (availableWidth === 0 || availableHeight === 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const scale = Math.min(availableWidth / originalWidth, availableHeight / originalHeight);\n    const displayWidth = Math.floor(originalWidth * scale);\n    const displayHeight = Math.floor(originalHeight * scale);\n\n    userImage.style.width = displayWidth + \"px\";\n    userImage.style.height = displayHeight + \"px\";\n}\n\n/**\n * Recompute the fitted image size when the viewport changes (e.g. iOS safe-area\n * adjustments that complete after the image was first sized).\n */\nfunction refitUserImageToContainer() {\n    const userImage = document.getElementById(\"user-image\");\n    const container = document.querySelector(\".image-container\");\n    if   width: 40px;\n    height: 40px;\n    padding: 9px;\n    border-radius: 9px;\n    flex-shrink: 0;\n    cursor: pointer;\n    border: none;\n    background: transparent;\n    color: #292929;\n}\n\n/* In-card CTA — the \"Next\"/\"Submit\" button on the active question screen.\n   Design role is Button (Accent, M): accent blue when ENABLED (the user\n   has selected an option) and gray when DISABLED (no option selected yet).\n\n   DCMAA-98102 root cause: the previous version forced #E6E6E6/#222222 on\n   BOTH the base and `:disabled` selectors (plus opacity:1 /\n   pointer-events:auto to defeat Jot's fade), so enabled and disabled\n   rendered identically and the accent blue never appeared.\n\n   State-detection note (verified in the WebView via DevTools console):\n   the submit <button> itself carries NO selection signal — no `disabled`\n   property/attribute, no `aria-disabled`, and its class list is identical\n   before and after a selection (`sc-… jot-viewer-quiz-submit-button`). So\n   a `:disabled` / attribute selector on the button cannot work. This Jot\n   build also does NOT emit the `.selectedAnswer` class pre-submit\n   (document.querySelectorAll('.selectedAnswer').length is 0 after a tap).\n   The one reliable signal is the native radio's `:checked` state — tapping\n   an option checks its <input type=\"radio\"> (checked-radio count goes\n   0 → 1). The checked radio and the submit button share the\n   `.jot-viewer-quiz-container` ancestor (confirmed by walking the checked\n   radio's ancestry), so we detect the enabled state with\n   `.jot-viewer-quiz-container:has(input[type=\"radio\"]:checked)` and paint\n   the button accent blue; the base rule below is the default (no-selection)\n   gray. `:has()` is WebView 105+ (Android 13+); on older WebViews the\n   enabled rule is a no-op and the button stays gray (graceful degradation)\n   — the same fallback posture used by the wrong-answer `:has()` rule above.\n\n   Geometry (quiz-designs Button M): h 40, radius 20, Adobe Clean 17/700\n   line-height 21. Padding 8/20/11 is on the inner text frame; on the bare\n   <button> here we use shorthand padding 0 20. Accent blue #0265DC light /\n   #0367E0 dark with #FFFFFF text matches the home Start button (see\n   .jot-viewer-quiz-start-button below). No-selection state keeps the\n   gray-200 pill: #E6E6E6/#222222 light, #3F3F3F/#EBEBEB dark. */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-quiz-submit-button {\n    height: 40px !important;\n    padding: 0 20px !important;\n    border-radius: 20px !important;\n    border: none !important;\n    font-family: 'AdobeClean', sans-serif !important;\n    font-size: 17px !important;\n    font-weight: 700 !important;\n    line-height: 21px !important;\n    display: inline-flex !important;\n    align-items: center !important;\n    justify-content: center !important;\n    background: #E6E6E6 !important;\n    color: #222222 !important;\n}\n\n/* Enabled state: an option is selected (native radio :checked) → accent\n   blue (Button Accent M). */\n@supports selector(:has(*)) {\n    .client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-quiz-container:has(input[type=\"radio\"]:checked) .jot-viewer-quiz-submit-button {\n        background: #0265DC !important;\n        color: #FFFFFF !important;\n    }\n}\n\n/* Home Start button (Button Accent M). quiz-designs(-light): bg #0265DC\n   light / #0367E0 dark, color #FFFFFF, h 40, radius 20, text Adobe Clean\n   17/700 line-height 21, padding 8/20/11 on text frame. Vertical padding is zero because the inter-element spacing\n   between question text and the first option is owned by the .jot-field-group-element\n   `gap` rule below.\n   The flex-grow/shrink lockdown is explicit (not just `flex: 0 0 auto`) so a\n   higher-specificity Jot rule that sets flex-grow:1 individually still loses. */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-quiz-question-area {\n    flex-grow: 0 !important;\n    flex-shrink: 0 !important;\n    flex-basis: auto !important;\n    display: flex !important;\n    flex-direction: column !important;\n    align-items: stretch !important;\n    padding: 0 !important;\n    box-sizing: border-box !important;\n}\n\n/* Question text element. width:100% forces horizontal fill — without it, the inner\n   element defaults to its content width in some Jot layouts, leaving the text in a\n   visually-narrow column even though the wrapper has room. margin-bottom:0 cancels\n   Jot's default 40px push (the wrapper's own bottom padding handles the gap to the\n   next sibling now). */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-quiz-question-area .jot-instructions-element {\n    margin-bottom: 0 !important;\n    width: 100% !important;\n    max-width: 100% !important;\n}\n\n/* ── Quiz question and option font sizes (mobile) ── */\n\n/* Question text. Single-line options look fine\n   (line-box defaults give a small natural break) but multi-line options\n   stack with NO gap because their wrapped lines flow continuously.\n   Forcing the wrapper to flex-column + gap:5px restores consistent\n   visible spacing between consecutive options regardless of single- or\n   multi-line text. without overflow-wrap the label\n   expands past the card edge on long options. line-height 1.25 matches Figma's\n   Component/L/Regular spec (20px line on 16px font); the previous 1.5 was a guess that\n   left options ~24px tall and stretched the row above the design's 40px. */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-field-group label {\n    line-height: 1.25 !important;\n    overflow-wrap: break-word !important;\n    word-break: break-word !important;\n    max-width: 100% !important;\n}\n\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-viewer-field-group label > span {\n    white-space: normal !important;\n    overflow-wrap: break-word !important;\n    word-break: break-word !important;\n}\n\n/* ── Quiz option selected / answer state colors ── */\n\n/* Actual Jot DOM structure (confirmed from DevTools 2026-05-11):\n       <span class=\"jot-data-field-container [selectedAnswer] [correctAnswer]\">    ← outer\n         <div class=\"form-field-edit\">\n           <label class=\"jot-radio-container [selectedAnswer] [correctAnswer]\">    ← inner\n             <input type=\"radio\" ...>\n             <span class=\"jot-label-element\">Option text here<\/span>               ← text\n           <\/label>\n         <\/div>\n       <\/span>\n   State classes are camelCase: .selectedAnswer, .correctAnswer (and assumed\n   .incorrectAnswer for wrong selections) — NOT the kebab-case .selected/.correct\n   I originally guessed from generic CSS naming conventions.\n   State classes appear on BOTH the outer .jot-data-field-container AND inner\n   .jot-radio-container, so targeting either works; using both makes the override\n   robust if a future Jot build moves the class to a single level.\n   Inner span (.jot-label-element) carries Jot's own color declarations, so a\n   plain container-level `color` does not cascade — we target it explicitly. */\n\n/* Unselected option — color #292929 (Spectrum gray-900, neutral default) per Figma\n   \"Quiz Choice - Mobile\" Text spec (all four choices on screen 3 share this color).\n   Selection cue in Figma comes from the radio fill, not from dimming the unselected\n   text — previous #c6c6c6 made every unpicked option look disabled. Applies in both\n   pre-submit and post-submit phases for options Jot has not flagged with a state class. */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container:not(.selectedAnswer):not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer),\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container:not(.selectedAnswer):not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) .jot-label-element,\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container:not(.selectedAnswer):not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) label,\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container:not(.selectedAnswer):not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) *:not(input) {\n    color: #292929 !important;\n}\n\n/* Pre-submit: user's selected option — dark readable text. .selectedAnswer is added\n   the moment the user taps an option; if they have not submitted, the option has\n   .selectedAnswer but neither .correctAnswer nor .incorrectAnswer.\n   Figma screen 3 (active question, pre-submit) shows all four choice text runs in\n   #292929 (Spectrum gray-900), e.g. I620:47225..28;69:22043;22027:615. The .selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer) rule\n   further below adds strikethrough+dark-gray styling that visually communicates the\n   wrong-answer state regardless — those declarations layer on top of this color rule. */\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer),\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) .jot-label-element,\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) label,\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-data-field-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) *:not(input),\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-radio-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer),\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-radio-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) .jot-label-element,\n.client-jot-overrides .jot-radio-container.selectedAnswer:not(.correctAnswer):not(.incorrectAnswer) *:not(input) {\n    color: #292929 !important;\n}\n\n/* Post-submit: correct answer — dark accessible green. Figma node\n   I620:47474;69:22090;22027:639 = \"Introduction of horses and new diseases\" rendered in\n   #014c34 (Spectrum green-900). Matches Figma exactly. Jot adds .correctAnswer to the\n   right answer (also paired with .selectedAnswer if the user picked it). Strikethrough communicates \"you picked this\n   and it was wrong\"; the dark-gray (rather than red) text keeps the visual hierarchy\n   muted relative to the .correctAnswer green which is the answer the user should read.\n   The radio fill color is set on `.incorrectAnswer input` further below via accent-color.\n\n   Selector strategy: we cannot confirm whether Jot actually applies the .incorrectAnswer\n   class — DevTools captures so far only show .selectedAnswer and .correctAnswer. Note:\n   that fallback also matches the pre-submit selected-but-not-yet-submitted state, so it\n   is layered AFTER the pre-submit rule and accepts that strikethrough may flash on the\n   user's tap before submit. // 20s\n    const TIMEOUT_FOR_NETWORK_STABLE_CHECK = 500; // 500ms check\n    const MAX_TIMEOUT_FOR_DOC_LOAD = 30000; // 30s\n    const TIMEOUT_FOR_DOC_STABILITY = 2000;\n\n    /**\n     * Stats object to track the time taken for each step in the conversion process.\n     */\n    stats = {\n        image_load_time: 0,\n        num_images: 0,\n        page_scroll_time: 0,\n        num_pages: 0,\n        document_load_time: 0,\n        styles_update_time: 0,\n        network_wait_time: 0,\n        total_modification_time: 0,\n        image_load_completed: false,\n        page_scroll_completed: false,\n        start_time: Date.now()\n    };\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to determine if the current webpage is loaded from a local file or not.\n     * @returns Boolean value indicating if the current webpage is loaded from a local file or not.\n     */\n    function IsLocalFile() {\n        return window.location.protocol == 'file:';\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to determine if the current webpage contains SCRIPT tags or not.\n     * @returns Boolean value indicating if webpage contains SCRIPT tags\n     */\n    function DoesContainScripts() {\n        return Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('script')).length > 0;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Determines if we need to perform extended optimizations on the webpage. This is required to ensure that the PDF conversion process is smooth and performant.\n     * @returns Boolean value\n     */\n    function ShouldPerformExtendedOptimizations() {\n        return !IsLocalFile() || DoesContainScripts();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Determines if a given element has printable content or not.\n     * @param {DomNode} ele Dom element to check if it has content or not\n     * @returns Boolean value indicating if the element has content or not.\n     */\n    function HasContent(ele) {\n        try {\n            // Either is image or has text content\n            if (ele && ele.tagName == 'IMG') {\n                return true;\n            }\n\n            if (ele && ele.tagName == 'IFRAME') {\n                return true;\n            }\n\n            return (ele && ele.textContent !== undefined && ele.textContent.trim().length > 0);\n        } catch (e) {\n            return true;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Determines if a given element is visible or not based on the following conditions:\n     * 1. Element has non-zero width and height\n     * 2. Element is not outside the viewport\n     * 3. Element is not hidden or display:none\n     * 4. Element is not fixed and has an offsetParent\n     * 5. Element has no children or children are not visible or have content\n     *\n     * @param {DomNode} ele Dom element to check if it is visible or not\n     * @param {Number} depth Depth of the element to check for visibility. Default is -1 which means all children are checked.\n     * @returns Boolean value indicating if the element is visible or not.\n     */\n    function IsVisibleElement(ele, depth = -1) {\n        try {\n            const bounds = ele.getBoundingClientRect();\n            if (bounds.width == 0 || bounds.height == 0) {\n                return false;\n            }\n\n            // if element is outside the viewport\n            if ((bounds.left < 0 && bounds.right < 0) || (bounds.top < 0 && bounds.bottom < 0) || (bounds.left > window.innerWidth && bounds.right > window.innerWidth)) {\n                return false;\n            }\n\n            const styles = window.getComputedStyle(ele);\n            if (styles.display == 'none' || styles.visibility == 'hidden') {\n                return false;\n            }\n\n            // non-fixed elements are visible if they have an offsetParent\n            if (styles.position !== 'fixed' && ele.offsetParent === null) {\n                return false;\n            }\n\n            // Check if any of the children are visible and has text content or images\n            if (depth != 0) {\n                const children = Array.from(ele.children);\n                for (let i = 0; i < children.length; i++) {\n                    if (IsVisibleElement(children[i], depth == -1 ? -1 : depth - 1)) {\n                        return true;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            return (depth == 0 || children.length == 0) && HasContent(ele);\n        } catch (e) {\n            return true;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to notify the native app that the document has been loaded.\n     */\n    function NotifyDocumentLoaded() {\n        SendToNativeApp({\n            \"event\": EVENTS.DOCUMENT_LOADED\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to notify the native app that the document has been loaded.\n     */\n    function NotifyScriptLoaded() {\n        SendToNativeApp({\n            \"event\": EVENTS.Script_LOADED\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to notify the native app to start the conversion process.\n     */\n    function InvokeConversion() {\n        SendToNativeApp({\n            \"event\": EVENTS.START_CONVERSION\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to add print-color-adjust style to all elements on the page. This is essential for printing background colors on wkwebview.\n     * This breaks the conversion on windows as it removes the header on wikiwand. This is not required on windows.\n     * \n     * This will not work on webpages with CSP policies prohibiting the execution of arbitrary styles.\n     */\n    function AddPrintBackgroundStyles() {\n        // This ensures that we are printing background on wkwebview\n        const style = '@media all { * { -webkit-print-color-adjust: exact !important; }}';\n        const styleElement = document.createElement('style');\n        styleElement.innerHTML = style;\n        document.head.appendChild(styleElement);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to get all the important elements on the page. This is required to ensure that we are able to capture all the content on the page.\n     * This will get all the text nodes and images on the page.\n     * \n     * @returns Set of all important elements on the page\n     */\n    function GetAllImportantElements() {\n        let importantRects = new Set();\n\n        function traverse(node) {\n            if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) {\n                // const rect = node.parentElement.getBoundingClientRect();\n                importantRects.add([node.parentElement, node.parentElement.getBoundingClientRect()]);\n            } else if (node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && (node.nodeName == 'IMG' || node.nodeName == 'IFRAME')) {\n                // check if image, then insert\n                importantRects.add([node, node.getBoundingClientRect()]);\n            } else {\n                Array.from(node.childNodes)\n                    .filter(child => IsVisibleElement(child, 0)) // Only consider current element for visibility check\n                    .forEach(child => traverse(child));\n            }\n        }\n\n        traverse(document.body);\n        return Array.from(importantRects);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function determines if the given element is overlapping with other content on the page. This is required to ensure that the PDF does not have any overlapping content.\n     * We will only consider it if this is not overlapping over text and images. Otherwise, we will ignore this element.\n     * \n     * @returns Method to determine if the element is overlapping with other content on the page\n     */\n    function IsOverlappingOverContentMethod() {\n        const allElementRects = GetAllImportantElements();\n\n        return ele => {\n            try {\n                const bounds = ele.getBoundingClientRect();\n                const overlappingElements = allElementRects.filter(([otherEle, otherBounds]) => {\n                    if (otherEle == ele || ele.contains(otherEle)) return false; // ignore self and children\n                    return bounds.left < otherBounds.right && bounds.right > otherBounds.left && bounds.top < otherBounds.bottom && bounds.bottom > otherBounds.top;\n                });\n\n                // Check if this element is overlapping over text or images\n                return overlappingElements.length > 0;\n            } catch (e) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        };\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to remove all fixed elements from the page. These elements are styled as position: fixed and would be visible\n     * on each page of the printed PDF. We will remove these elements to ensure that the PDF looks clean and does not have any overlapping content.\n     * This uses inline-style element to perform this operation which would break if the CSP policy does not allow unsafe-inline styles.\n     * \n     * This also removes all the invisible elements from the page. This is required to ensure that the PDF does not have any hidden content.\n     */\n    function RemoveFixedElements(allBodyElements, checkForOverlap) {\n        const allElements = allBodyElements.filter(ele => ele.position == 'fixed');\n        allElements.forEach(ele => {\n            if (ele) {\n                let isVisible = true;\n                try {\n                    do {\n                        const bounds = ele.bounds;\n                        if (bounds.width == 0 || bounds.height == 0) {\n                            isVisible = false;\n                            break;\n                        }\n\n                        // if element is outside the viewport\n                        if ((bounds.left < 0 && bounds.right < 0) || (bounds.top < 0 && bounds.bottom < 0) || (bounds.left > window.innerWidth && bounds.right > window.innerWidth)) {\n                            isVisible = false;\n                            break;\n                        }\n\n                        if (ele.display == 'none' || ele.visibility == 'hidden') {\n                            isVisible = false;\n                            break;\n                        }\n\n                        // non-fixed elements are visible if they have an offsetParent\n                        if (ele.position !== 'fixed' && ele.element.offsetParent === null) {\n                            isVisible = false;\n                            break;\n                        }\n\n                        isVisible = ele.element.children.length > 0 || HasContent(ele.element);\n                    } while (false);\n                } catch (e) { }\n\n                const existingStyles = ele.element.getAttribute('style') || '';\n                if (isVisible) {\n                    if (checkForOverlap(ele.element)) {\n                        try {\n                            // If this is overlapping over other content, then we should reflow it.\n                            ele.element.setAttribute('style', existingStyles + ';position:relative !important; overflow:unset !important;');\n\n                            // Remove from their current heirarchy\n                            if (ele.element.parentNode) {\n                                ele.element.parentNode.removeChild(ele.element);\n                                ele.element.parentNode.appendChild(ele.element);\n                                // document.body.appendChild(ele.element);\n                            }\n                        } catch (e) { }\n                    } else {\n                        ele.element.setAttribute('style', existingStyles + ';position:absolute !important; overflow:unset !important;');\n                    }\n                } else {\n                    // Remove from their current heirarchy\n                    ele.element.setAttribute('style', existingStyles + ';display: none !important;position:absolute !important;');\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to enable overflow on the body. This is done as some webpages block the scroll operation\n     * on the webpage to show a particular popup or modal. We will enable the overflow to ensure that the page can be scrolled.\n     * This uses inline-style element to perform this operation which would break if the CSP policy does not allow unsafe-inline styles.\n     */\n    function EnableBodyOverflow() {\n        try {\n            document.body.setAttribute('style', document.body.getAttribute('style') + ';overflow: visible !important;height: auto !important;position:unset !important;');\n            document.documentElement.setAttribute('style', document.documentElement.getAttribute('style') + ';overflow: visible !important;height: auto !important;position:unset !important;');\n        } catch (e) { }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to remove all the scripts from the page.\n     * We will do this to ensure that the scripts do not interfere with the conversion process.\n     */\n    function RemoveAllScripts() {\n        const allScripts = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('script'));\n        allScripts.forEach(script => {\n            if (script) {\n                script.parentNode.removeChild(script);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to remove all the iframes from the page. The Iframes are usually used to embed content from other websites\n     * and could cause issues with the conversion process. We would not have access to the content of the iframe and hence we will remove\n     * all the iframes from the page. This would remove all ads from the page.\n     */\n    function RemoveAllIframes() {\n        const allIframes = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('iframe'));\n        allIframes.forEach(iframe => {\n            if (iframe) {\n                iframe.parentNode.removeChild(iframe);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Determines if we are running inside an iframe or not.\n     */\n    function IsInsideIframe() {\n        try {\n            return window.self !== window.top;\n        } catch (e) {\n            return true;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to remove all the print styles from the page. This is required to ensure that the print styles are not applied\n     * to the page. We will remove all the print styles and apply the screen styles to the page. This would ideally give us the same output\n     * as the browser view.\n     */\n    function RemoveAllPrintStyles() {\n        // There could be 3 cases for including print styles\n        // 1. links with media='print'\n        // 2. styles with media='print'\n        // 3. CSSMediaRule with media='print'\n\n        // There are two ways to determine if a style is applied for print state\n        // 1. Explicitely styles defined with media='print'\n        // 2. Styles defined only for screen but not for print\n        try {\n\n            // We want the screen styles to be inserted for print styles as well\n            Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('link')).forEach(link => {\n                if (link.media.includes('print')) {\n                    link.setAttribute('media', link.media.replaceAll(/(only )?print/gi, 'none'));\n                } else if (link.media.includes('screen')) {\n                    link.setAttribute('media', link.media.replaceAll(/(only )?screen/gi, 'all'));\n                }\n            });\n\n            Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('style')).forEach(style => {\n                if (style.media.includes('print')) {\n                    style.setAttribute('media', style.media.replaceAll(/(only )?print/gi, 'none'));\n                } else if (style.media.includes('screen')) {\n                    style.setAttribute('media', style.media.replaceAll(/(only )?screen/gi, 'all'));\n                }\n            });\n\n            // This is not complete as we are not able to access the cssRules for the stylesheet due to CORS policy\n            const styleSheets = Array.from(document.styleSheets);\n            styleSheets.forEach(styleSheet => {\n                try {\n                    if (styleSheet.cssRules) {\n                        Array.from(styleSheet.cssRules)\n                            .filter(rule => rule instanceof CSSMediaRule)\n                            .forEach(rule => {\n                                // We are only looking at first media query instead of all media queries. This needs to be fixed.\n\n                                // This is only working for media starting with print. This does not take into account the following\n                                // media queries where print and screen are combined.\n                                // example: @media (max-width: 800), print {} -> This is additive and should only remove the print media.\n                                // example: @media (max-width: 800) and print {} -> This is combined and should remove all styles.\n                                // example: @media only screen {} -> This should be renamed to all to alow printing to use this style.\n                                if (rule.media && (rule.media.mediaText.includes('print') || rule.media.mediaText.includes('screen'))) {\n                                    const mediaText = rule.media.mediaText.replaceAll(/(only )?screen/gi, 'all').replaceAll(/(only )?print/gi, 'none');\n                                    rule.media.mediaText = mediaText;\n                                }\n                            });\n                    }\n                } catch (e) {\n                    // we are not allowed to access the cssRules for this stylesheet due to CORS policy\n                }\n            });\n        } catch (e) {\n            // Ignore if we cannot completely perform the style modifications\n            console.error(e);\n        }\n\n        // stats.styles_update_time = Date.now() - stats.start_time - stats.document_load_time;\n    }\n\n    /*\n    * This function is used to reflow all absolute elements to unset.\n    * This is required to ensure that the absolute elements are not overlapping with other elements.\n    * We will reflow all absolute elemnts which do not have correct hierarchy in the DOM meaning they\n    * do not have offsetParent.\n    */\n    function ReflowAbsoluteElements() {\n        const allElements = Array.from(document.body.querySelectorAll('*')).filter(ele => ele.offsetParent == null);\n        allElements.forEach(ele => {\n            if (!ele)\n                return;\n            const s = window.getComputedStyle(ele);\n            if (s.position == 'absolute') {\n                ele.setAttribute('style', (ele.getAttribute('style') ? ele.getAttribute('style') + ';' : '') + 'position:unset !important;');\n                // Remove from their current heirarchy\n                if (ele.parentNode) {\n                    ele.parentNode.removeChild(ele);\n                }\n\n                // Also move them at the end of body\n                document.body.appendChild(ele);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Determines the correct image source for the given PICTURE element. This is required as the PICTURE element can have multiple sources.\n     * We will determine the correct source based on the width of the image and the width of the source.\n     * \n     * @param {DomNode} ele Dom element to determine the image source\n     * @returns link to the image source\n     */\n    function DetermineImageSource(ele) {\n        try {\n\n            // Do not modify the images for local files\n            if (IsLocalFile()) {\n                return ele.getAttribute('src');\n            }\n\n            // Check if this image parent is Picture element\n            let sources = [];\n            if (ele.parentNode.tagName == 'PICTURE') {\n                // Picture element can have multiple sources with each source having a srcset attribute\n                sources.push(...Array.from(ele.parentNode.children).filter(ele => ele.tagName == 'SOURCE').map(ele => ele.getAttribute('srcset') || ele.getAttribute('data-srcset')).filter(src => src && src.trim().length > 0));\n            } else if (ele.tagName == 'IMG') {\n                const source = (ele.getAttribute('srcset') || ele.getAttribute('data-srcset') || '').trim();\n                if (source.length > 0)\n                    sources.push(source);\n            }\n\n            if (sources.length == 0) {\n                return ele.getAttribute('src');\n            }\n\n            // We are only picking the first available source.\n            // Ideally the srcs are split with comma (,) but on some webpages, the URLs also contains comma (,)\n            // const firstSource = sources[0].split(' ').map(src => src.trim()).filter(src => src);\n\n            // Iterate over the srcset and determine the image with maximum width/height constraint\n\n            // The structure of the srcset is as follows:\n            // <url> <constraint>,<url> <constraint>,...\n            const src = sources[0].trim();\n\n\n\n            // Determine the image with maximum width/height constraint\n            let selectedSource = '';\n            let maxSourceSize = 0;\n\n            let currentSource = '';\n            let currentSourceSize = 0;\n            let lastIndex = 0;\n            for (let i = 0; i < src.length; i++) {\n                if(src[i] == ' ') {\n                    currentSource = src.substring(lastIndex, i).trim();\n                    lastIndex = i+1;\n                } else if(src[i] == ',' && currentSource.length > 0) {\n                    currentSourceSize = parseInt(src.substring(lastIndex, i).trim());\n                    lastIndex = i+1;\n\n                    if(currentSourceSize > maxSourceSize) {\n                        maxSourceSize = currentSourceSize;\n                        selectedSource = currentSource;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            return maxSourceSize > 0 ? selectedSource : ele.getAttribute('src');\n        } catch (e) { }\n\n        return ele.getAttribute('src');\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Simple heuristic to determine if the given image is eligible for tracking or not. This is required to ensure that we only track images.\n     * @param {HTMLElement} ele HTML image element\n     * @returns Boolean whether we should track this image for loading or not\n     */\n    function IsImageEligibleForTracking(ele) {\n        try {\n            if (!(ele && ele.tagName == 'IMG' && ele.width > 50 && ele.height > 50))\n                return false;\n\n            // Check if the image has a valid source\n            const src = ele.getAttribute('src');\n            if (!src || src.trim().length == 0)\n                return false;\n\n            return true;\n        } catch (e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to load all the images on the page. This is required to ensure that all images are loaded before we start the conversion process.\n     * This handles the cases for lazy loading images as well. We will force load all images on the page.\n     * \n     * @param {Number} timeout Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for all images to load\n     * @returns Promise that resolves when all images are loaded or timeout is reached\n     */\n    async function LoadAllImages(timeout = MAX_TIMEOUT_FOR_IMAGE_LOADING) {\n        return new Promise((resolve) => {\n            // Track if we have already sent the message\n            let hasSentResponse = false;\n\n            // Track the number of images on the page\n            let trackedImages = 0;\n\n            // Timeout for image load fallback\n            let timeoutHandle = -1;\n\n            // Helper method to notify parent when loading is completed\n            function loadCompletionHelper() {\n                if (!hasSentResponse) {\n                    // Notify parent that we have processed all images\n                    SendToNativeApp({\n                        \"event\": EVENTS.IMAGED_LOADED\n                    });\n\n                    hasSentResponse = true;\n                    // stats.image_load_completed = trackedImages == 0;\n                    // stats.image_load_time = Date.now() - stats.start_time - stats.document_load_time;\n                    resolve();\n\n                    if (timeoutHandle >= 0) {\n                        window.clearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            function setImageLoaded() {\n                trackedImages--;\n\n                if (!hasSentResponse && trackedImages <= 0) {\n                    loadCompletionHelper();\n                }\n            }\n\n            function setImageUnloaded() {\n                trackedImages++;\n            }\n\n            // fallback in case we get stuck on loading image for a long time\n            timeoutHandle = setTimeout(loadCompletionHelper, timeout);\n\n            // Iterate through all the images and track their loading progress\n            const allImages = Array.from(document.body.querySelectorAll('img'))\n                .filter(ele => IsImageEligibleForTracking(ele));\n            // stats.num_images = allImages.length;\n            allImages.forEach(img => {\n                setImageUnloaded();\n\n                // Forcefully load all images without user interaction\n                img.setAttribute('loading', 'eager');\n                img.setAttribute('decoding', 'sync');\n\n                // Attach callback for load response\n                img.onload = setImageLoaded;\n                img.onerror = setImageLoaded;\n\n                const selectedSrc = DetermineImageSource(img);\n\n                // Force reload of all images\n                img.setAttribute('src', selectedSrc);\n                img.setAttribute('srcset', selectedSrc);\n                img.removeAttribute('sizes');\n            });\n\n            // In case there are no images on the page\n            if (allImages.length == 0) {\n                loadCompletionHelper();\n            }\n        });\n\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to scroll through all the pages on the webpage. This is required to ensure that all the content is loaded and\n     * we are able to capture all the content on the webpage. This function will scroll through all the pages on the webpage and notify the\n     * native app when all pages are scrolled.\n     * \n     * @param {Number} maxPages Maximum number of pages to scroll\n     * @param {Number} pageDuration Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for each page to render between scrolls\n     * @param {Number} timeout Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for all pages to scroll\n     * @returns Promise that resolves when all pages are scrolled or timeout is reached\n     */\n    async function ScrollAllPages(maxPages = MAX_PAGES_TO_SCROLL, pageDuration = PER_PAGE_SCROLL_DURATION, timeout = MAX_TIMEOUT_FOR_PAGE_SCROLL) {\n        return new Promise((resolve) => {\n            // Each page segment to scroll\n            const pageHeight = window.innerHeight || DEFAULT_PAGE_SCROLL_HEIGHT;\n\n            // Max scroll height to go to\n            const maxScrollheight = maxPages * pageHeight;\n\n            // Track the current scroll height\n            let currentScrollHeight = 0;\n\n            // Track if we have already sent the message\n            let hasSentResponse = false;\n\n            // Timeout for image load fallback\n            let timeoutHandle = -1;\n\n            // Original URL of the webpage\n            const originalURL = location.href;\n\n            // Helper method to notify parent when scroll is completed\n            function scrollCompletionHelper() {\n                // Scroll back to top\n                window.scrollTo(0, 0);\n\n                if (!hasSentResponse) {\n                    // Notify parent that we have processed all images\n                    SendToNativeApp({\n                        \"event\": EVENTS.PAGE_SCROLLED\n                    });\n\n                    hasSentResponse = true;\n                    // stats.page_scroll_time = Date.now() - stats.start_time - stats.document_load_time;\n                    resolve();\n\n                    if (timeoutHandle >= 0) {\n                        window.clearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            // fallback in case we get stuck on loading image for a long time\n            timeoutHandle = setTimeout(() => scrollCompletionHelper, timeout);\n\n            // Helper method to scroll to next page\n            function scrollHelper() {\n                // Maximum height of page we are allowed to scroll to\n                const maxPageHeight = Math.min(document.documentElement.scrollHeight, maxScrollheight);\n                // stats.num_pages++;\n\n                // Early break in case we have already sent scroll event\n                if (hasSentResponse) return;\n\n                const currentURL = location.href;\n\n                /** \n                 * In case of dynamically loaded articles webpages, the URL changes when a new articles is loaded. \n                 * This will let us know that we have processed the content for the original article. This however does\n                 * result in more content than expected as the content for next article is already loaded.\n                 **/\n                if (currentScrollHeight >= maxPageHeight || currentURL != originalURL) {\n                    // we have reached the end of webpage\n                    // stats.page_scroll_completed = true;\n                    scrollCompletionHelper();\n                } else {\n                    currentScrollHeight += pageHeight;\n\n                    // Scroll to next page\n                    window.scrollTo(0, currentScrollHeight);\n\n                    // Request to move to next page\n                    setTimeout(scrollHelper, pageDuration);\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Start the scrolling process\n            scrollHelper();\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This function is used to add conversion stats to the page. This is used for debugging purposes to track the time taken for each step in the conversion process.\n     */\n    function AddConversionStats() {\n        const statsEle = document.createElement('pre');\n        statsEle.style.display = \"block\";\n        statsEle.style.width = \"100vw\";\n        statsEle.style.height = \"1px\";\n\n        // format stats object in stats div\n        // statsEle.innerHTML = JSON.stringify(stats, null, 2);\n\n        document.body.appendChild(statsEle);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Heuristic based fixes for incorrect page break issue on some pages.\n     * example: https://trends.google.com/trending?geo-us&geo=IN\n     * example: https://www.cricbuzz.com/cricket-news/132382/ct-25-snowballing-into-a-crisis-as-pcb-confirm-bcci-refusal-to-play-in-pak\n     */\n    function DetectIncorrectPageBreaks(allElements) {\n        try {\n            // Print operation inserts a page break after each element which is specified as inline-block or inline-flex and\n            // spans over multiple pages. This is not the case with block elements. We will convert all inline-block and inline-flex\n            // elements to block elements to ensure that they are not broken across pages.\n            const inlineBlockElements = allElements.filter(ele => {\n                return (ele.display == 'inline-block' || ele.display == 'inline-flex' || ele.display == 'flex') && ele.bounds.height >= DEFAULT_PAGE_HEIGHT;\n            });\n\n            // Convert all inline-block and inline-flex elements to block elements\n            inlineBlockElements.forEach(ele => {\n                ele.element.setAttribute('style', (ele.element.getAttribute('style') ? ele.element.getAttribute('style') + ';' : '') + 'display:block !important;');\n            });\n        } catch (e) { }\n    }\n\n    function RemoveImageSourceset(allElements) {\n        try {\n            const allImages = allElements.filter(ele => ele.element.nodeName == 'IMG');\n            allImages.forEach(img => {\n                img.element.removeAttribute('srcset');\n                img.element.removeAttribute('sizes');\n                img.element.removeAttribute('loading');\n                img.element.removeAttribute('decoding');\n            });\n        } catch (e) { }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Heuristic based fixes for quality issues in the conversion process.\n     */\n    function HeuristicBasedFixes(body) {\n        try {\n            const checkForOverlap = IsOverlappingOverContentMethod();\n            const allElements = Array.from(body.querySelectorAll('*'));\n\n            // Get all elements with shadow dom\n            const allShadowBodies = [body, ...allElements.filter(ele => ele.shadowRoot).map(ele => ele.shadowRoot)];\n\n            allShadowBodies.forEach(childBody => {\n                const allShadowElements = (childBody == body ? allElements : Array.from(childBody.querySelectorAll('*')))\n                    .map(ele => {\n                        const styles = window.getComputedStyle(ele);\n                        return {\n                            element: ele,\n                            position: styles.position,\n                            display: styles.display,\n                            visibility: styles.visibility,\n                            offsetParent: ele.offsetParent,\n                            bounds: ele.getBoundingClientRect()\n                        }\n                    });\n\n                // Remove any overlapping and duplicated content\n                RemoveFixedElements(allShadowElements, checkForOverlap);\n\n                if (IsLocalFile()) {\n                    RemoveImageSourceset(allShadowElements);\n                }\n                //ReflowAbsoluteElements();\n\n                // Heuristic based improvements\n                DetectIncorrectPageBreaks(allShadowElements);\n            });\n\n            // Add conversion stats\n            // stats.total_modification_time = Date.now() - stats.start_time;\n            // This can throw exception on TrustedHTML pages where insertion is not allowed.\n            // AddConversionStats();\n        } catch (e) { }\n        SendToNativeApp({\n            \"event\": EVENTS.DOC_MODIFIED\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Override network callbacks to track if requests are running before invoking print on it\n     */\n    const realfetch = window.fetch;\n    const realXMLSend = XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send;\n    const realXMLOpen = XMLHttpRequest.prototype.open;\n    window.requestsRunning = 0;\n\n    window.fetch = function (...args) {\n        window.requestsRunning++;\n\n        const req = realfetch.apply(this, args);\n        req.finally(_ => { window.requestsRunning--; });\n        return req;\n    }\n\n    XMLHttpRequest.prototype.open = function (...args) {\n        this._url = args;\n        return realXMLOpen.apply(this, args);\n    }\n\n    XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send = function (...args) {\n        window.requestsRunning++;\n\n        this._onreadystatechange = this.onreadystatechange;\n        this._onloadend = this.onloadend;\n        this._onabort = this.onabort;\n        this._onerror = this.onerror;\n\n        this._already_counted = false;\n\n        this.onloadend = function () {\n            if (!this._already_counted) {\n                window.requestsRunning--;\n                this._already_counted = true;\n            }\n\n            if (this._onloadend && this._onloadend != this.onloadend)\n                this._onloadend.apply(this, arguments);\n        };\n\n        this.onabort = function () {\n            if (!this._already_counted) {\n                window.requestsRunning--;\n                this._already_counted = true;\n            }\n\n            if (this._onabort && this._onabort != this.onabort)\n                this._onabort.apply(this, arguments);\n        };\n\n        this.onerror = function () {\n            if (!this._already_counted) {\n                window.requestsRunning--;\n                this._already_counted = true;\n            }\n\n            if (this._onerror && this._onerror != this.onerror)\n                this._onerror.apply(this, arguments);\n        };\n\n        this.onreadystatechange = function () {\n            if (this.readyState == 4 && !this._already_counted) {\n                window.requestsRunning--;\n                this._already_counted = true;\n            }\n\n            if (this._onreadystatechange && this._onreadystatechange != this.onreadystatechange)\n                this._onreadystatechange.apply(this, arguments);\n        };\n\n        return realXMLSend.apply(this, args);\n    }\n\n    async function isNetworkStable(timeout = MAX_TIMEOUT_FOR_NETWORK_STABLE) {\n        return new Promise(resolve => {\n            let isResolved = false;\n            let isNetworkStable = false;\n\n            const check = () => {\n                if (isResolved) return;\n\n                if (window.requestsRunning == 0) {\n                    // Give extra 500ms to ensure that network is stable\n                    // and we do not get any new requests\n                    if (isNetworkStable) {\n                        isResolved = true;\n                        SendToNativeApp({\n                            \"event\": EVENTS.NETWORK_STABLE\n                        });\n                        resolve();\n                        return;\n                    }\n\n                    isNetworkStable = true;\n                } else {\n                    isNetworkStable = false;\n                }\n\n                setTimeout(check, TIMEOUT_FOR_NETWORK_STABLE_CHECK);\n            };\n\n            check();\n            setTimeout(() => {\n                if (!isResolved) {\n                    isResolved = true;\n                    SendToNativeApp({\n                        \"event\": EVENTS.NETWORK_STABLE\n                    });\n                    resolve();\n                }\n            }, timeout);\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * This is the main function that will be called to perform pre-processing and start the conversion process.\n     * @returns Promise that resolves when conversion process is completed\n     */\n    async function Main() {\n        if (window.calledOnload) return;\n        NotifyDocumentLoaded();\n\n        window.calledOnload = true;\n        // stats.document_load_time = EnableBodyOverflow();\n\n            if (ShouldPerformExtendedOptimizations()) {\n\n                // Scroll through the pages to allow for lazy loading of images and content\n                await
